I’m trying to say about the keys, because that’s the main thing, but then my mouth is already on the jail part, and then I’m thinking of the prayer rug, and chess pieces too—the knight, how it doesn’t go straight.\n\nAnd people say they can’t follow me. They look at me like I skipped a whole page, but in my head it connects somehow. I’m not trying to confuse anybody. It just comes out all mixed up.\n\nDoctor: Do your thoughts ever feel like they’re slipping away or that you can’t hold onto one idea before another one comes in?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it does that. I’ll try to hold one thing, like the keys, because that’s what I meant to explain, and then it slips sideways into the jail thing, and then I’m already talking about the prayer rug, and then chess pieces—like the knight, how it jumps funny.\n\nPeople tell me they can’t follow what I’m saying, and I get frustrated because it makes sense for a second in my head, but when I say it out loud it’s all mixed up.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being put into your mind by someone else, taken out of your mind, or broadcasted so that others can hear them?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I don’t think so. Like, I have thoughts that come in fast, and then I lose the first one, like when you’re sweeping and somebody leaves footprints right behind you, but I don’t know about somebody putting them in there.\n\nBroadcasted… you mean like people can hear what I’m thinking? I don’t think I’ve noticed that. People don’t understand me when I talk, mostly. That’s the part I know.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced things that others around you don’t seem to notice—like hearing voices when no one is there, or seeing things that others say aren’t real?\n\nPatient: Sometimes… yeah. I hear this man’s voice, like it’s coming out of the vents, or sometimes by the mop closet. It’ll say my name, or say I ruined everything.\n\nAnd I know vents make noise, especially in old buildings, they rattle and carry sound, like when people talk near the stairwell and it comes out wrong on another floor… but this sounds real. Like he’s right next to me, not far away. It makes me stop what I’m doing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs that others don’t share and that turn out not to be true, like thinking someone is following you or that you have a special connection to something most people wouldn’t understand?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess. There was this thing at the building, with the hallway lights. Not like regular flickering, because I know when bulbs are bad, I change those, or I tell maintenance, depending on which floor it is.\n\nBut I felt like the building people had marked me, like they all knew something about me. My supervisor even showed me the camera, said, “Look, nobody’s there, nothing’s happening,” but it didn’t really… it didn’t settle it. I still felt sure they were using the lights and these little looks in the hallway, like warnings to each other about me.\n\nAnd the funny thing is the cameras never catch the part you need, you know? They catch a cart sitting there for three hours but not the one second when somebody gives you that look. So yeah… people said it wasn’t true, but I couldn’t shake it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by an outside force, removed from your mind, or that your thoughts are being broadcasted so that others can hear them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like somebody sticking a thought in my head, no. My thoughts get crowded, like too many brooms in the closet and you can’t pull one out without everything falling over, but that’s just… that’s my head, I guess.\n\nAnd taken out—sometimes I forget what I was saying right in the middle, but that happens when I’m tired, or when somebody interrupts me, like the elevator bell going off and then I lose the whole thing. I don’t think anyone’s pulling them out.\n\nBroadcasted… I don’t know. I don’t think people can hear them. If they could, they’d probably tell me to slow down, because even I can’t keep up sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being controlled by something outside of yourself, like someone is putting thoughts into your mind, pulling them out, or that your thoughts are being heard by others around you?\n\nPatient: Not like thoughts, exactly… I don’t know about somebody putting them in or taking them out. But with the building, it felt like everybody already knew something about me.\n\nLike I told you, even when my supervisor showed me the camera and there was nothing there, I still felt sure the building people had marked me somehow. The hallway lights, the way they’d flicker, and then somebody gives you this little look—like when people pass notes without actually passing notes, you know? They were using that to warn each other about me.\n\nAnd cameras… they miss things. They always miss the part that matters. So no, not my thoughts being controlled maybe, but I felt like they were all in on something about me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are not your own, like someone is putting new thoughts into your mind, taking your thoughts away, or that your thoughts are being heard by others around you?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t think of it like that. Like, my thoughts are mine, I guess, even when they get all mixed up and I can’t grab the right one.\n\nSometimes I’ll be mopping the lobby and I start thinking about one thing, then the bucket wheel squeaks, and suddenly I’m thinking about whether I left the storage room unlocked, and then about my brother’s old truck—so it jumps around. But that’s not somebody putting it there, I don’t think.\n\nAnd people hearing them… no, I haven’t really noticed that. If they hear anything, it’s probably me talking under my breath, because I do that when I’m trying to remember what I’m doing. But thoughts just out loud in the air? I don’t know. I don’t think so.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are not your own, like someone is putting new thoughts into your mind, taking your thoughts away, or that your thoughts are being heard by others around you?\n\nPatient: I’m still not sure I know what you mean by not my own. I don’t sit there and think, “that came from somebody else,” if that’s what you’re asking.\n\nSometimes I lose the thread, but that’s more like when you’re cleaning and you got the cart squeaking and somebody asks where the bathroom is and then the paper towels are out, and by the time you turn around you can’t remember what you were thinking. That happens to everybody, I think.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think anyone’s putting thoughts in or taking them out. And I haven’t noticed people hearing them. Not like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had the experience of thoughts being put into your head that don't feel like your own, or thoughts being taken out, or that your thoughts are being heard by others around you?\n\nPatient: Not really like somebody putting thoughts in my head. I keep saying that part wrong, maybe. It’s more… I knew people in the building knew something about me.\n\nEven after my supervisor showed me the camera and said there wasn’t anything there, I didn’t believe it all the way. Cameras don’t catch everything, and the hallway lights would flicker at certain times, and then somebody would give me a look—just a small look—and it felt like they had marked me somehow. Like they were warning each other about me without saying it.\n\nSo not thoughts being pulled out or heard, no… but it felt like they all had a message going around about me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had the experience of thoughts being put into your head that definitely didn't come from you, like they were inserted by someone or something else, or felt like your thoughts were being pulled out of your mind or heard by other people around you?\n\nPatient: No… I mean, I don’t think so. I know my head gets busy, and sometimes I’ll be halfway down the hall and forget why I went that way, but that’s not like somebody reached in and took it. It’s just… too much going on.\n\nAnd inserted, like planted there? I don’t know. I’ve had strange thoughts, sure, everybody does, especially when you’re tired and the floor machine is making that loud hum for an hour. You start thinking about lunch, then the old school I went to, then whether the trash bags are the right size… but it still feels like me thinking it.\n\nPeople hearing them, no. If folks know something, it’s probably from watching or talking, not hearing what’s inside my head. I don’t think they can hear it like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things happening around you, like people’s actions or events, were specifically directed at you or had a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, that’s more what I mean. Like in the building, it wasn’t just regular stuff happening. The hallway lights would flicker and then somebody would glance over at me, real quick, like they knew. Like they were passing something along.\n\nMy supervisor, he showed me the camera and said, “See, nothing’s there,” but cameras don’t show everything. I used to clean that hallway, I know where the blind spots are, and the lights in that place are old anyway, they buzz and blink, and maintenance never fixes it right. But it still felt… directed at me. Like they had marked me somehow, and the little looks and the lights were warnings to each other about me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things people do around you—like their actions or words—are secretly about you or meant to send you a message?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s what I’m trying to say. Like, not every single thing, but in that building it felt like people were doing things around me that meant something.\n\nMy supervisor showed me the camera and said nothing was on there, but I still didn’t feel right about it. I know those hallways, I cleaned them. The lights would flicker, and then somebody would look at me quick, and it felt like they were warning each other. Like I’d been marked somehow.\n\nAnd people act like lights just flicker, you know, old wiring, maintenance never fixes anything right… but it wasn’t just the lights. It was the looks with it. It felt like it was about me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being put into your mind by someone or something else, taken out of your mind, or that others can hear your thoughts even when you’re not speaking them out loud?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it’s like that. I get thoughts running around, like when you’re working and you got the mop bucket, the trash, somebody asking you where the bathroom is even though the sign’s right there… your mind jumps.\n\nBut I don’t feel like somebody put a thought in there. And I don’t think they’re pulling them out. If people know something, it’s probably because I said too much or they saw my face. I’ve been told I don’t hide things good. My sister used to say that too, back when we were kids—well, not kids kids, but younger.\n\nBut hearing my thoughts? No… I don’t think people can hear them if I’m not saying them. That would be something else.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had the experience where you felt that thoughts were being placed into your mind that didn’t come from you, or that your own thoughts were being removed, or that other people could hear your thoughts even when you weren’t speaking them out loud?\n\nPatient: No, not like somebody putting thoughts in or taking them out. It’s more… I felt sure people in the building had marked me somehow. Even when my supervisor showed me the camera and said nothing was there, I still didn’t believe it.\n\nThe hallway lights would flicker, and then somebody would give me this little look, quick, like they were warning each other about me. I know people say old lights do that—those lights were always bad—but it felt connected. Like it was meant for me."
